# monty.composition

## Reply/history migration review gate

The existing packaged `host-post-reply` and `host-save-history` Recipes, their
associated Skills and PythonCode are migration inputs, **not qualified current-v3
implementations to reuse unchanged**. Updating these exact workflows is the parent
task; implementing the qualified preload/export adapter is its prerequisite.

Before selecting a successor, independently review the actual retained definitions,
interfaces, bindings, dependency graph, typed input/result handoffs and global-runtime
callers against `recipe.md`, `skills.md` and section 24 of `monty.composition.md`.
Upgrade the usages and Recipes for effect-free dependency-first loading, explicit
pinned export invocation, isolated mutable state and current kernel enforcement.
Complete the applicable exact-combination bootstrap/approval and behavioral evidence
before activation. Names, existing package status, old acceptance receipts and the
word “canonical” do not qualify the unchanged implementation.

Preserve compatible stable UUIDs and old task selections when that reviewed migration
supports them; do not copy deprecated source substitution, ambient step state,
module-level Tool execution or unsafe retry guidance into the new adapter. Review
reply/history ownership, both routing branches, history framing, finalization and
no-replay behavior together after the adapter is qualified. Keep these internal
usages out of public MCP discovery unless a separately qualified public contract
explicitly permits exposure. The commented instructions below are historical only.


**MCP source status (2026-10-09):** the raw-Skill/composer serving stub has been retired and legacy outbound MCP activation/attachment has been suspended. Earlier source observations of that stub are historical. See [section 26](#26-inbound-mcp-source-review-and-transport-retirement-2026-10-09) for the repairs and the still-required Recipe/chat/Kohai implementation. The new inbound service is not yet runtime-qualified.

**Updated v3 Skill definition:** a Skill is one Tool usage exposed through a preloadable Python function interface, with prose, explicit associated code, public exported names/signatures and private helper/constant dependencies. Resolve and pin its exact interface/code/association/Tool graph, load definitions in dependency-first order without effects, and invoke the selected export on demand. Loading enables no Tool; matching ToolSkill binding and live kernel checks remain required. Code/immutable constants can be shared; mutable arrays/defaults/closures/results are isolated per invocation/task/attempt. See [the binding function-interface contract](../../skills.md#preloadable-function-interface-binding-v3-target). All legacy and new Skill rows require the per-usage function migration in the implementation appendix; pure wrappers remain PythonCode documentation. This is a target and plan update, not runtime support.

## Tier-2 history and Sempai handoff audit

No-Match turns require the same verified reply/history completion as Match turns.
The former early return after publication was a bug, not an architectural exemption.
The root source now converges both routes on reply verification followed by the
retained history Recipe. Failure after publication must retain that completed
effect and must not publish again or replay the task as Tier 2.

Daily-memory records, the authoritative conversation transcript and Sempai's
analysis input are distinct. The current resolved model gateway captures each
provider request's supplied history/prompt in a forensic packet and adds the
provider response afterwards. However, `InterceptingModelGateway::prepare`
performs Sempai review before Kohai dispatch, while `close` only records the
response. There is no post-response Sempai call in that path. Packet persistence
failures are logged and do not currently establish a durable learning handoff.
A packet status named `SempaiReviewed` therefore does not prove that Sempai has
reviewed the completed current turn. The legacy `PgKohaiPort` also records packets,
but its source explicitly has no Sempai dispatch; it is not an alternative that
completes this requirement.

The completed-turn learning workflow remains required implementation work:
review/compose a Recipe using qualified existing usages for a correlated typed
bundle of conversation/run/attempt/message identities, original and effective
model prompts, responses, Tool/effect receipts and terminal completion/failure
state. Pin the Sempai model/prefix selection independently. Record a durable,
deduplicated review request with exact bundle references, submit any proposed
components through the authored Q1/behavior/human-Q2 path, and retain review and
queue outcomes. Delivery/review recovery may not replay task effects, publish a
second reply or infer absent effects from an incomplete packet. Resolve missing
primitive/store/runner support explicitly before activation; a daily log or an
existing pre-dispatch review is not a substitute for this post-turn workflow.

**Producer implementation:** V122 now retains an immutable review event in the
original admission settlement transaction for actual No-Match turns, including
failed turns after publication. It snapshots available model packets and exact
Recipe/Tool evidence without private claim/admission keys. This is durable
handoff retention, not delivered Sempai review or proof of complete task data.
The event explicitly has `evidence_complete=false`. The consumer, qualified
model/proposal usages and durable review acknowledgement remain prerequisites.
Follow the [completed-turn Recipe authoring record and acceptance contract](../reborn/contracts/completed-turn-sempai-review.md).

**Model evidence implementation:** V127 and the resolved host interceptor retain
original/effective request JSON separately, including explicitly captured provider
Tool replay metadata that ordinary message serialization omits. Returned response
JSON preserves structured output and usage; returned preparation/provider errors
retain classified unresolved evidence. New settlement events snapshot this column.
This repairs a consumer prerequisite; it does not qualify complete turn evidence,
provide raw provider wire bytes, enable automatic retry, or implement delivery.

## 1. Legacy components containing Python code to update and convert

This list contains class-22 PythonCode components and their identified legacy Skill usage companions: **101 conversion candidates** after reviewing **134 distinct seeded PythonCode names**. **92 Skill companions** are grouped with their code so that a usage is converted once. Recipes, Actions, Tools, ToolSkills, prose-only overviews, catalogues and class-10 roots are excluded from the list. Each link identifies the seeder call or generated definition that installs that name; UUIDs are instance-assigned and must be resolved during implementation.

Under [skills.md §§1, 3 and 7](../../skills.md), a Skill comprises prose plus explicitly associated PythonCode; the stored prose is never executed. The 169 legacy Skill seed calls were inspected, including constant and inline bodies. They contain usage prose, signatures, data examples and code references, not separate executable entry points. The companions below are historical usage/reference mappings inferred from the seed bodies and surrounding workflows, **not verified persisted v3 associations**. Explicit pc references and matching one-Tool usage descriptions qualify; merely mentioning Python syntax or several Tools in an overview does not. The range Skill is covered by the foreseen interval usage; the separate parse Skill is covered by the typed JSON example, as recorded in section 3.

The follow-up [component-by-component coverage audit](monty.composition.coverage.md) checks every original entry and companion against later seeds and concrete plans. It moved nine validator-owned tasks and eight redundant dispatch bodies out of this list, retaining relevant Skill usage companions with canonical executors. Partial overlap leaves distinct contract work here; an existing Tool or a plan describing the same old component is not a replacement.

The short changes below supplement the common v3/Monty acceptance requirements in section 5. A fixed body with no placeholders may still need a new revision, usage association and result contracts. Section 3 records the 33 excluded PythonCode names and applicable Skill dispositions and their reuse destinations; “foreseen” means design/planned work, not an activated component.

## 2. Newer components containing Python code

The same three-column format below covers **20 newer executable components or explicitly planned executable roles**: **four concrete packaged Python-bearing components** and **16 target examples/planned roles**. Rows are numbered only for cross-review; the numbers are not component identities. Packaged reply/history identities are the actual catalogue keys, not new pc names. Associated Skills are grouped with their code. The protected class-10 root is included because it contains executable Python; it is not a child Skill usage, and its helper functions are not separate seeded components.

Authority: [recipe.md](../../recipe.md), [skills.md](../../skills.md), [validator_v3.md](../../validator_v3.md), [tools.md](../../tools.md) and [toolskills.md](../../toolskills.md). There is **no validator.md in this checkout**; validator_v3 is the inspected validator plan. Apply the common v3/Monty criteria in section 5 to every row. “No rewrite indicated” is source inspection, not a fresh execution/qualification claim. Every shipped change remains an immutable successor; package qualification and installed authoring follow their separate review paths.

The planned roles have an explicit Python/executable-usage requirement but often no source body yet; this list therefore records the implementation gap rather than fabricating code, component names, UUIDs or host APIs. Validator-class routes are Recipes and are not listed as code. The Python root is a separate protected architecture component. General Rust parsers/stores, tests, superseded basic-mode scripts, Actions, Recipes, ToolSkills and ordinary native CPython prefix compiler scripts are excluded as separate library components. The registered compiler’s small Monty calling usage is included; its native implementation is not.

**Overlap handling:** each newer row names identified old-code overlaps or related predecessors, including old entries already removed from section 1 and retained in section 3 dispositions. The old rows carry **Duplicate review** flags. Exact replacement, subset and infrastructure overlap are different; flags do not authorize deletion or re-selection of old artifacts. Old audit findings remain preserved in [the coverage report](monty.composition.coverage.md).

Monty-specific review uses the actual pinned interpreter and BrassClaw adapter, not CPython parsing alone. Verify suspension/resumption, declared typed boundary values and result extraction; avoid unsupported generator functions, structural matching and arbitrary imports. Async root ports and native-worker execution have different contracts from class-22 child steps. See [language/parser limitations](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/language/), [host functions](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/host-functions/) and [host-boundary values](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/host-values/).

## 3. Reuse decisions: excluded from the conversion list

These are dispositions of PythonCode names and their applicable Skill companions, not additional conversion candidates. The corresponding host lifecycle Skill wrappers reuse the current root/package boundary; skill-shell-date redirects to the canonical time usage. Prose-only multi-tool/domain overviews, safety checklists and usages with no identified executable body are outside this Python conversion list; missing executable associations remain separate library authoring work, not evidence that those Skills are complete. Exclusion means reuse or retire for **new selections**; it does not authorize deletion of retained revisions, operator overrides or running-task artifacts. A runtime primitive alone does not make all of its usages equivalent. General memory writes, substring filtering, nullable-path extraction and domain API shaping retain distinct contracts.

## 4. Evidence, scope and present execution behavior

This is a source audit of the working checkout on **2026-10-09**, including its existing local changes. It does not inventory operator-authored database rows or prove that this checkout is deployed. Before implementation, reconcile the installed instance's retained catalogue and overrides with these seed definitions. No components are activated, deprecated in storage or deleted by this plan.

The PythonCode inventory covers all named seed calls in [builtin_bootstrap.rs](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/builtin_bootstrap.rs), [seed_builtin_host.rs](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/seed_builtin_host.rs) and [zencoder_bootstrap.rs](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/zencoder_bootstrap.rs): 103 + 12 + 11 named bodies, plus eight generated `pc-validator-class-*` bodies. All 134 bodies were reviewed, including the host components' included Python files. The Skill audit covers 150 + 8 + 11 seed calls; inline bodies, referenced constants and the concatenated `doc-convert-method` overview were considered. That overview is workflow prose, not executable Python. Class-10 roots and their Python lifecycle programs are architecture evidence, not legacy library candidates here. Actions have been replaced by Recipes; neither belongs in the conversion list.

The ordinary global path is already Monty-based, but it does **not** compile every older seeded row into every program. [InstalledMontyCatalogue](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/installed_monty_catalogue.rs) constructs a limited installation-owned catalogue of pinned reply/history workflows, uses exact packaged definitions and retained implementations, and builds its prefix from those selected documents. [Packaged usages](../../crates/brassclaw_skills/src/global_bootstrap_components.rs) already use typed `inputs` and exact association records. [The global root](../../crates/brassclaw_engine/orchestrator/global_mode.py) drives matching, pinned execution, model work, reply posting and history through task-bound ports. These implementations explain the reuse decisions; they do not establish full-library admission.

Legacy bootstrap still seeds older rows. The [composer](../../crates/brassclaw_engine/src/memory/composition.rs) has both a typed retained path and a legacy substitution path. The older rows' presence is therefore neither proof of compatibility nor a universal deprecation flag. They remain relevant as capability definitions and migration material, but a row enters a new executable selection only after the retained catalogue, contracts, bindings and compiler admit it coherently. Existing task selections must keep their old artifacts.

The retained path preserves code separately from data and rejects unresolved source placeholders. [Retained source inspection](../../crates/brassclaw_engine/src/executor/retained_source.rs) checks every selected body, requires `result` assignment, checks allowed imports/intrinsics and currently accepts one direct matched host call or pure logic. Its dynamic-host and one-call restrictions are actual adapter limitations. The broader documented dependent-chain exception cannot be assumed available in this implementation.

The remaining library, authored activation and prefix work belongs to the existing [simplified v3 implementation plan](simplified-v3-implementation.md) and [prefix upgrade plan](prefix_v3_upgrade.md). This conversion plan contributes to their acceptance gates; it does not replace them or mark them complete.

## 5. Conversion criteria

### V3 usage and assembly contract

The binding authorities are [recipe.md](../../recipe.md), [skills.md](../../skills.md), [tools.md](../../tools.md) and [toolskills.md](../../toolskills.md), with the installation-seed exception and current lifecycle status in [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md). Apply their complete contracts, including these requirements:

- A Skill exposes one Tool usage through explicitly associated preloaded functions, with declared names/signatures/dependencies and exact task-pinned resolution. Reuse compatible code across several usages with separate reviewed argument/result contracts. Move broad overviews to Extension documentation and workflow sequencing to Recipes. Pure helpers remain PythonCode without an artificial ToolSkill or Skill wrapper.
- Each persisted component step contains one stable UUID, using the actual `knowledge`/`stepnumber` schema and exact input-reference grammar. A normal Tool usage has a Rust ToolSkill binding step immediately followed by its associated Orchestrator PythonCode. Independent calls need independent execution steps. Internal pure-code dependencies need explicit pinned compilation support.
- Bind step-local values through `inputs["local_name"]`. Remove source placeholders, quoted JSON slots and access to ambient `state`/previous-result variables. Declare recursive list/object contracts, extra-field rules, nullability, bounds and defaults. Missing and null differ; defaults apply only to missing consumer inputs. Typed producer results feed later consumers without stringification or source reconstruction.
- Keep stable component identities and create immutable successor revisions. A task-start catalogue snapshot pins the Recipe revision, variant, exact step link/input layout, complete code graph, associations, approvals and actual retained Tool implementations. Child execution, retries and resumes retain this selection.
- Association approval is separate from task selection and individual component validation. Record exact reviewed revisions/checksums and dependency combinations. Installed edits need authored Q1, behavior evidence and human Q2. Reviewed shipped successors use the controlled noncircular `system_seed` integrity/Q1/behavior path without extra installed-instance human Q2. A source/status label is insufficient.
- ToolSkill metadata neither executes nor grants access. Resolve actual Tool UUID, callable, capability and retained adapter; verify parameters/results before effects. Check live global Tool policy and technical constraints at every dispatch and retry, independently of fixed component versions.
- Preserve classified errors, cancellation, unresolved effects and supported explicit waits/handles; unknown completion is not confirmed failure or success. Persist positive attempt counts and dispatch/effect state. Retry only eligible outcomes with verified read-only or durable deduplication evidence. A timeout is not evidence that no effect occurred; completed effects never replay because later validation or posting failed. Only actual No-Match enters Tier 2. All shell and spawn-subagent Recipes are Tier 1.

### Monty parsing and semantics

Use the **actual BrassClaw-pinned Monty build** and retained adapter as the acceptance target. The vendored [Monty control crate](../../vendor/monty-control/crates/monty/Cargo.toml) and [upgrade evidence](../../tests/monty_v1_upgrade/README.md) identify the local integration; record its exact upstream revision, local patch/artifact checksum and effective settings in conversion evidence. Rolling upstream documentation may describe behavior beyond that artifact. A CPython syntax check alone is insufficient.

Monty parses/compiles source at construction and host calls suspend for host completion. Validate each exact retained body before effects and exercise the actual suspension/resumption path. Monty's last expression supplies its output; BrassClaw also requires assigned `result`. Verify the adapter's wrapper/result extraction rather than appending arbitrary source or assuming an assignment alone is a returned value. Keep immutable source and typed arguments separate. See the [Rust quickstart](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/quickstart/rust/) and [host function contract](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/host-functions/).

Check imports, builtins, keyword arguments, exception handling and comprehension behavior against supported Monty semantics. Generator expressions currently materialize lists; do not infer lazy streaming. Unsupported constructs such as `yield` generators, structural matching and asynchronous iteration/context managers need different implementations. Upstream `eval`/`exec` support is **not** permission to use them: BrassClaw's retained inspector rejects executable intrinsics and forbidden ambient access such as `os`, `subprocess` and `open`. See [language limitations](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/language/) and [eval/exec semantics](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/eval_exec/).

Monty's `re` implementation differs from CPython. Test UUID/name validation with full-string matching, including trailing newlines; test supported APIs instead of assuming `re` compatibility. Tool-side grep uses its own engine and needs separate evidence. Encode URLs from UTF-8 bytes, not Unicode code-point numbers. See [regular expressions](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/re/) and [encoding support](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/encoding/).

Monty `json` supports a limited parsing/serialization surface. Do not assume file-based load/dump, encoder/decoder classes or unsupported hooks. Parsing is not recursive schema approval; nonfinite numbers require rejection under the chosen usage contract. When duplicate-key rejection or other unsupported parsing semantics are required, use a verified strict host parser rather than inventing a Python API. See [JSON limitations](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/json/).

The host boundary must carry only the declared typed value shapes. Reject booleans where integer counts are required, out-of-profile numbers, cycles, opaque objects and unsupported/lossy conversions. Do not accept repr strings or depth-limit placeholders as ordinary valid application data. Preserve isolation across tasks/attempts and intermediate results across supported waits. See [host values](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/host-values/).

Enforce compute time, memory and structural limits through the shared service's supported accounts; awaiting a Tool is not executing VM time. Do not add artificial token caps when token budgets are disabled. Monty snapshots require compatible trusted integrity-checked data and do not establish durable effect reconciliation. Task failure must not trigger whole-Recipe replay or a replacement per-chat VM. See [resource limits](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/resource-limits/), [snapshots](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/snapshots/) and BrassClaw's stricter supervisor/recovery rules in [tools.md](../../tools.md).

## 6. Implementation sequence

1. **Resolve identities and reuse destinations.** Produce a machine-readable ledger from the source inventory and installed catalogue: old name/UUID/revision/checksum, historical Skill companion, actual association status, canonical destination, dependencies and disposition. Resolve the illustrative interval usage and current reply/history packages before redirecting callers. Inspect overrides and retained tasks. Do not interpret a matching name, class or validated flag as an approved association.
2. **Define usage contracts before rewriting bodies.** For each listed entry, reconcile prose, actual primitive parameters, fixed arguments and recursive input/result/failure contracts. Establish one-Tool usage boundaries and pure helpers. Split multi-call read/tail/filter, trigger removal and memory append only as needed by their actual semantics. Reuse one compatible dispatcher with multiple reviewed usage associations; do not create a duplicate executor for each fixed selector. Specify new host support only when an existing primitive/adapter truly cannot implement the operation.
3. **Complete required retained assembly support.** Verify catalogue selection, typed IBS bindings, exact associations, internal dependencies and loaded aliases in the real startup/worker path. Implement missing store/runner support before activating code that depends on it. Never bypass it by executing Skill prose, metadata or an arbitrary returned source string. Keep the current compiler's restrictions until explicit supported changes and acceptance justify expansion.
4. **Convert and qualify in manageable families.** Start with zero-host lexical/encoding/formatting helpers and the canonical interval usage. Then convert read-only filesystem/time/JSON/HTTP retrieval and domain shaping. Follow with effectful memory/file/trigger/API writes, shell usages and authoring validators. Each family includes its Skill prose corrections and exact combination evidence; a parse-only batch is not complete. Prefix/bootstrap lifecycle wrappers excluded in section 3 remain with their existing owners.
5. **Validate through real Monty and host bindings.** Compile exact successor bytes, execute representative valid/invalid inputs in the pinned interpreter, and then exercise retained assembly and actual registered adapters in a controlled environment. Verify input isolation, outputs, policy, effects, cancellation, waits and retained selection, including failures before dispatch. Reuse unchanged evidence only under the development policy's identity conditions.
6. **Ship immutable packaged successors and coherent catalogue generations.** Qualify installation-owned seeds with package/artifact integrity, structural/Q1 and behavioral evidence, using noncircular bootstrap ownership. Preserve operator overrides and old selections. Installed authoring uses its separate human-Q2 path. Catalogue activation occurs only after the full selected dependency/association combination and production runner support pass their gates; do not activate incompatible rows and silently omit them during matching.
7. **Retire legacy execution admission after acceptance.** Redirect relevant Recipe callers to canonical UUIDs using the actual persisted schema. Prevent placeholder-composed or prose-derived programs from entering execution, including older composer/MCP surfaces. Remove old defaults only after selection and resume tests show that new tasks use approved successors while old tasks retain their artifacts. Retiring new admission is distinct from deleting historical rows or implementations.

## 7. Required acceptance evidence

These are implementation checks to perform later, **not tests run or passed by this planning task**. Follow [the development policy](../development-policy.md): choose targeted evidence after coherent changes, avoid repeated blanket builds, and expand only for unresolved failures or risks.

| Area | Evidence required |
| --- | --- |
| Inventory and deduplication | Every audited PythonCode name is either a conversion entry or an explained reuse/retirement. Each companion has an explicit disposition and no second executable is added for the same compatible usage. Installed-only rows/overrides are reconciled separately. |
| Source/data separation | Quotes, backslashes, newlines, braces, literal placeholder-looking text and injection strings remain exact data. Source checksum stays unchanged across different inputs. Unresolved source slots fail before dispatch. |
| Recursive values | Missing/default/null, false/zero, nested lists/objects, unknown fields, malformed items, boolean-as-count, overflow and nonfinite values follow declared contracts. Invalid producers never acquire consumer defaults or become empty-success results. |
| Filesystem | Actual offset/limit and total_lines behavior, empty/EOF/default-cap/truncated reads, relative/root lexical paths, missing versus denied/binary/failed reads, supported grep modes and absent invert support are exercised. Tail and filtering handoffs use exact typed results. |
| Encoding and parsing | `é` becomes `%C3%A9`; reserved characters and whitespace have declared behavior. UUID/name validation rejects trailing newlines. JSON syntax and schema failures differ, present null differs from absent paths, and regex behavior is verified in the correct engine. |
| Shell | Commands receive preserved arguments, no option injection or implicit stage-all default. Commit messages with quotes/newlines survive correctly. Exit failures and platform-specific unavailable commands remain failures. All callers are Tier 1; environment/config/process output follows secret-handling rules. |
| HTTP and external API | Real callable/alias and body/envelope shapes, pagination, authentication, non-2xx and malformed payloads pass controlled tests. Missing plans are not generic successful empty task responses. Completed or unknown POST/PATCH effects cannot replay blindly. |
| Skill and validator semantics | Companion prose agrees with executable behavior and real Tool contracts. Narrow nonempty-field validators are not treated as association, behavioral or activation approval. Pure helpers do not masquerade as one-Tool Skills. |
| Retained selection and global execution | A multi-step converted workflow executes through the ordinary global service with typed data, pinned references and isolated task state. New revision activation during a wait does not change the suspended or child task's selection. Cancellation/policy changes fence subsequent dispatches. |
| Failure and recovery | Preflight errors cause zero effects and never Tier-2 fallback. Durable attempt counts, stale fencing and confirmed/unresolved effects survive supported recovery; failed outputs/replies do not repeat completed writes. No per-chat replacement VM or whole-Recipe replay appears. |
| Bootstrap and activation | Exact packaged artifacts, noncircular trusted provenance and required combination evidence are verified. Upgrades preserve overrides and old artifacts. Full-library/current authored admission remains incomplete until the existing v3 plan's production gates pass. |

## 8. Completion boundary

The work is complete when each candidate has a qualified immutable successor or a documented canonical reuse destination, its relevant Skill companion is aligned and explicitly associated, and new selections execute that exact approved combination through the production retained Monty path. Remaining unconverted entries must have explicit implementation dispositions before any catalogue generation containing them is activated. Documentation, parsing success and seed insertion alone do not meet this boundary.

Keep the limited reply/history production evidence separate from full-library acceptance. This plan specifies conversion and evidence; it does not claim that all v3 runtime/store requirements or complete prefix compilation are already implemented.

## 9. Follow-up coverage findings

See [the complete per-component audit](monty.composition.coverage.md) for all 118 original PythonCode entries and 94 original Skill companions, including entries removed after the second pass. Nine tasks already belong to validator_v3; eight specialized dispatch bodies can reuse existing general code. The remaining entries have distinct or unresolved contracts, even when their primitives or part of their behavior are shared. No later fully qualified drop-in executable replacement was found for a listed body.

## 10. Goal: reusable PythonCode assembled by Recipes

Build a large library of **Recipes composed from small reusable PythonCode units**, rather than a large library of complex task-specific Python programs. A PythonCode unit performs one coherent transformation or one declared Tool usage. A Recipe supplies the task workflow: input capture, preparation, Tool binding/calls, data handoff, branching where supported, completion and failure handling. Small does not mean one line: keep validation and an operation's necessary local processing together when splitting would produce fragments with no independent reuse.

A split is justified only when a resulting part has another named consumer here or a credible concrete future usage, with a stable useful input/result contract. Do not create a component for every comparison, concatenation or private function. Reuse the same immutable code across compatible Skills/Recipes, and keep different usage contracts explicit. Fixed command/pattern/endpoint values usually become reviewed Recipe data, not a separate executable. Pure logic is PythonCode, not a zero-Tool Skill. A Skill remains one Tool usage with prose and an associated preloadable function interface, declared exports/dependencies and pinned invocation; a Recipe coordinates multiple usages. The protected global root remains a lifecycle component.

The component-by-component assessment is in section 11. [Concrete implementation sections](monty.composition.implementation.md) cover every retained legacy entry, every newer entry, their Skill companions and every proposed reusable extraction. Proposed helper labels are design handles, not allocated names or UUIDs. Resolve an existing compatible component first; allocate a new stable UUID only if none exists. A replacement workflow retires the monolith from new admission after acceptance, while keeping old task-selected artifacts.

### 10.1. Design the Recipe so IBS can assemble the right program

1. State the goal and observable completion. Classify effects and Tier: deterministic eligible workflows can be Tier 0; shell/spawn-subagent and explicit creative model work are Tier 1. Define captured inputs and recursive result schemas before arranging steps.
2. Resolve canonical components. Each persisted component step has exactly one stable UUID in `include`. Tool use normally has a `knowledge: "rust"` ToolSkill step immediately followed by the matching `knowledge: "orchestrator"` PythonCode step. Pure logic has only the executable step. Independent Tool calls are separate pairs; internal pure composition is allowed only with pinned dependencies and supported assembly.
3. Bind typed inputs separately from source. Use whole metadata references such as `{{vars.path}}`; each code unit accesses a local `inputs["path"]`. A preceding result/field supplies the next local input through the typed layout. Never paste a value, JSON string, arithmetic expression or prior result into Python source. Consumer defaults apply only to missing inputs.
4. Use the actual `step_descriptions` / `stepnumber` / `knowledge` / `type: "component"` schema and a selected variant's exact `step_link`. `channel`, `step_id` and ad hoc flow fields are not insert fields. `type: "text"` annotates; it does not execute. The typed layout below is a distinct versioned layout document, not an invented field added to a legacy step.
5. IBS and composition select one approved catalogue generation, retain the Recipe/variant/layout and all exact revisions/checksums, associations, dependencies and actual Tool artifacts, then create the ephemeral BuildInstruction and prepared program references. Recipes hold stable UUIDs; selection supplies exact versions. Matching, assembly, child execution and continuation retain the same selection.
6. Keep intermediate results owned by the task in Monty. Validate each producer before a consumer reads it, preserve missing versus null, and propagate failures. Bindings grant no permission; the kernel checks live global Tool policy before dispatch. Missing support or an incompatible approved combination is an assembly error, never a No-Match or permission to downgrade.
7. Qualify the complete Recipe combination and production path. Planned layouts, associations, adapters and flow support must exist before activation. Example Python parsing does not establish Monty, behavior or bootstrap acceptance. Installation seeds use reviewed noncircular system_seed qualification; installed edits use authored Q1/behavior/human-Q2.

### 10.2. Concrete composition example: read and select literal lines

Use the existing read usage, the proposed reusable literal-line filter and packaged reply. The source never changes when the path or substring changes. Read returns the actual numbered display content; this profile filters that display text. A raw-source search needs a qualified raw-content result or the canonical grep Tool instead. A default-capped read means the reply covers only those returned lines; a Recipe promising the whole file must obtain complete input and verify completeness first.

The following is the exact persisted step **shape**, with symbolic UUID tokens to resolve before authoring. It is a design template, not insertable JSON with invented component identities. The Recipe's descriptions and intent variants must follow recipe.md, including its intent-example requirements.

```json
{
  "name": "read-and-select-literal-lines",
  "description": "Filter the numbered text returned by one default-cap read; report only that returned selection.",
  "step_descriptions": [{
    "desc_idx": 0, "label": "read and select literal lines", "yaml_source": "",
    "steps": [
      {"stepnumber": 1, "knowledge": "rust", "goal": "Bind file read", "content": "", "type": "component", "include": ["<READ_TOOLSKILL_UUID>"]},
      {"stepnumber": 2, "knowledge": "orchestrator", "goal": "Read supplied file", "content": "", "type": "component", "include": ["<READ_CODE_UUID>"]},
      {"stepnumber": 3, "knowledge": "orchestrator", "goal": "Select literal lines", "content": "", "type": "component", "include": ["<LINE_FILTER_UUID>"]},
      {"stepnumber": 4, "knowledge": "orchestrator", "goal": "Format selected lines", "content": "", "type": "component", "include": ["<LINE_REPLY_FORMATTER_UUID>"]},
      {"stepnumber": 5, "knowledge": "rust", "goal": "Bind reply publication", "content": "", "type": "component", "include": ["<REPLY_TOOLSKILL_UUID>"]},
      {"stepnumber": 6, "knowledge": "orchestrator", "goal": "Publish answer", "content": "", "type": "component", "include": ["<REPLY_CODE_UUID>"]}
    ]
  }],
  "variants": [{"variant_key": "literal-lines", "description": "Literal substring filter over the returned numbered display text", "step_link": "0:1-0:6", "intent_examples": ["read % and select lines containing %", "read file % and keep lines containing %", "show lines from % containing %", "find lines in % with literal %", "filter file % for text %", "select matching lines in % using text %", "read % then filter by literal %", "show the returned lines of % containing %", "preview % and keep lines with %", "select lines from % containing %"], "variable_patterns": [{"name": "path", "pattern": null, "description": null}, {"name": "substring", "pattern": null, "description": null}]}]
}
```

In the retained Recipe document, store the next layout under `input_layouts["literal-lines"]`; this is already the packaged constructor/parser shape, not an attribute added to individual steps. The separate input-layout profile uses the same `task_input` / `result` / `constant` reference forms inspected in the packaged constructor and retained-input parser. It is selected/pinned with the workflow; support for each field/path must be verified in the general catalogue path before admitting this Recipe.

```json
{
  "format": "recipe-input-layout/1",
  "task_inputs": {"path": {"type": "string", "required": true, "checks": [{"kind": "min_length", "value": 1}]}, "substring": {"type": "string", "required": true, "checks": []}},
  "steps": {
    "0:2": {"path": {"kind": "task_input", "reference": "{{vars.path}}"}},
    "0:3": {
      "content": {"kind": "result", "step_id": "0:2", "path": ["content"]},
      "substring": {"kind": "task_input", "reference": "{{vars.substring}}"},
      "invert": {"kind": "constant", "value": false}
    },
    "0:4": {"lines": {"kind": "result", "step_id": "0:3", "path": []}},
    "0:6": {"answer": {"kind": "result", "step_id": "0:4", "path": []}}
  }
}
```

Required composition yields read binding → unchanged read source with typed path → unchanged filter source with validated content/substring/invert → unchanged line formatter → reply binding → unchanged reply source with formatted answer. It must not join these into a source string containing the filename or file contents. Binding steps do not create user-visible results. The final reply reference remains task-owned; packaged history follows the supported finalization path. Read/filter failure causes zero reply publication and no Tier-2 replay.

The [line formatter implementation](monty.composition.implementation.md#proposed-line-reply-formatter) is a proposed reusable pure component for filtered text, tail previews and other bounded line reports. It keeps the empty-selection behavior explicit instead of letting the reply Tool receive whitespace-only text.


### 10.3. Exact local handoffs for split usages

These names are local inputs and result paths, not Python-source templates. Store them using the versioned layout grammar above and resolve all component identities. Constants use `kind: constant`; fields use `kind: result` with the named producer step and a string-field path. Do not assume a supported general branch or error-to-data mechanism when it is absent.

| Compound usage | Required typed handoff |
| --- | --- |
| Tail read | First call read_file(path, offset=1, limit=1) through a qualified bounded-read profile; bind its actual total_lines → tail-window.total_lines, constant50 → count. Bind window.offset/window.limit and original path → a qualified offset/limit read profile. This profile must allow limit0 for empty files; the inclusive start/end profile alone does not. Do not change source or fabricate line_count. These two reads are not an atomic file snapshot: define the result as a best-effort window derived from an earlier read or add a qualified revision/handle contract; never claim snapshot-tail consistency when files can change between calls. |
| Read then filter / literal inverse | Bind read.content → filter.content, captured substring → substring and reviewed constantfalse/true → invert. Output list → line formatter.lines. Record read.truncated_by_default; partial output cannot satisfy a whole-file promise. Regex inverse is a distinct unresolved contract. |
| Directory filter | After a qualified structured list adapter exists, bind entries → records.rows, constants name/contains or type/equals → field/mode, captured substring or reviewed type → value. Preserve all entry fields. Current display strings do not satisfy that input contract. |
| Trigger lookup/removal | Bind actual list.triggers → records.rows, name/equals → field/mode, captured name → value; filter list → require-one.rows; selected trigger_id → remove.trigger_id. Supply require-one.complete only from trusted complete-query evidence. Current trigger_list is capped at100, lacks completeness/pagination metadata and accepts limit rather than scope; its output alone cannot prove name uniqueness. Validate the schema and identity/race semantics before selecting by name. Return remove acknowledgement, not removed=True synthesized from dispatch. |
| Plan create/read/patch | Captured slug → plan-target.slug. Its scalar result → write.target/read.path/patch.target. Pass prepared plan content or exact old/new marker strings separately. Replacement fixes append=False only for replace-capable targets; patch fixes replace_all=False unless reviewed otherwise. Missing plan is a classified read outcome. |
| Parameterized shell | Explicit validated argument list → shell-quote.arguments; quoted list → command-specific internal builder; command string → shell-dispatch.command. Commit/wc have one argument; add has an explicit nonempty path list; push has remote and ref; pull has remote and optional ref. Validate options/refs before quoting. No implicit stage-all. |
| API requests | Each original path/query value → URL encoder.text or UUID validator.uuid. Encoder.encoded/canonical UUID → path-builder.encoded_segments; query list uses exact key/value pairs, with missing filters omitted. Relative path → zencoder-get.path or zencoder-write.path; fixed POST/PATCH selector and separately validated/serialized body → write.method/body. For GitHub prefix with fixed origin, resulting full URL → canonical HTTP url, with qualified auth/header/result compatibility. |
| Task summary | Actual successful task/plan envelopes → validated task and steps. steps → step-counts.steps; counts plus same steps/task → summary. A plan-not-created branch supplies an explicitly approved absent-plan output, never defaults a failed plan to an empty successful list. |
| Prefix validation | Trusted snapshot/source manifest and native build coverage facts → prefix-coverage.expected_source_refs/covered_source_refs. Pass the same trusted subject_ref and facts_complete; reject duplicate expected and covered references before set comparison. Its result is only coverage evidence; the Recipe also requires integrity, compiler/parser/tokenizer and capacity checks before publication. |
