## 12. Concrete implementation instructions

Read [monty.composition.implementation.md](monty.composition.implementation.md) for individual sections covering all 101 legacy entries, all 20 newer entries, every identified Skill companion, and each proposed split component. Each section specifies prose to retain/store, typed inputs/results, executable or explicitly limited example code, composition/reuse and implementation caveats. Missing host adapters are marked as blockers; preparation examples must never be installed as successful executors. This is the implementation specification, not an activation or runtime acceptance claim.

## 13. Correctness, security and architecture audit (2026-10-09)

Audited the main plan, historical coverage report and implementation appendix against recipe.md, skills.md, tools.md, toolskills.md, validator_v3.md, current retained input/source parsers and actual file/grep/JSON/HTTP/memory/shell/trigger handlers. Rechecked upstream Monty language, host values, re, JSON and UTF-8 encoding documentation. Findings below are corrected in the affected plan text/examples; missing runtime support remains explicitly blocked implementation work. Nothing was implemented or activated by this audit.

| Finding | Plan correction and required acceptance |
| --- | --- |
| Incomplete Recipe example input schemas | Added required and checks to both captured inputs, kept layout under the selected variant, and retained symbolic UUIDs as non-insertable identities. Final authoring must also include complete Recipe/variant metadata, negative routing cases and verified slot/capture transport. |
| Unsupported generic schema shapes | Removed mixed string/integer path lists in favor of homogeneous tagged objects and required concrete per-usage JSON/record/HTTP profiles. No invented any/union/enum/pattern/max_items fields. Additional bounds/selectors require supported checks and code/adapter validation. |
| Association requirements were too generic | Added exact stable association and separate approval-record completion gates, complete direct/fixed/computed argument accounting, immutable closure/artifact checksums, trusted Q1/behavior/Q2 or qualified seed provenance, and assembly pinning. Each Skill prose draft must be specialized; inferred companion names remain unverified. |
| Trigger parameter mismatch and unsafe name lookup | Actual calls use limit and trigger_id, not scope/trigger_name. Name removal requires trusted complete lookup; the capped100 list lacks that evidence. Remove by selected ID, handle no match/ambiguity/races, and preserve actual removed/nullable trigger acknowledgement. Legacy scoped backend support does not become a final-v3 role requirement. |
| Source/result contracts misstated | Corrected shell output/exit_code/success/sandboxed, bare JSON parse values, string-reparsing stringify/query behavior, directory display strings and HTTP header/status/truncation profiles. Bind successful payloads rather than runner envelopes; no fabricated success or incomplete-data defaults. |
| Validator aggregation could accept wrong-subject or empty evidence | Each check now carries subject_ref; partial examples have complete=False and passed=False. Aggregation rejects empty/duplicate required sets, duplicate checks, wrong subjects, incomplete/unevidenced and contradictory passes. Trusted evidence resolution remains outside pure code. |
| Set comparisons could hide duplicates | Graph examples reject duplicate input refs; prefix coverage rejects duplicates in expected and covered refs, incomplete facts and empty expected subjects. Full graph checks still require unique UUID/revision selection and retained actual Tool artifacts, not strings alone. |
| Generic filter accepted invalid mode for empty lists and bool==1 | Validate selector before looping; require concrete per-usage field/value contracts and type-compatible equality. A pure helper cannot turn malformed producer data into an empty successful result. |
| URL builder accepted malformed escapes/raw controls | Validate canonical uppercase percent escapes/unreserved strings and reject literal dot segments/raw delimiters. Validate original endpoint identifiers before encoding and retain kernel egress/redirect/secret enforcement. Encoding does not grant permission. |
| Header formatter silently changed escaping grammar | Replaced unqualified JSON quoting with exact inspected legacy header shape under a restricted quote/backslash/control-free name profile. Broader Unicode/escaped-name support requires shared compiler-format qualification; no silent native format change. |
| Whitespace selection was reported as no matches | Empty list and nonempty whitespace-only list now have distinct display messages while substantive text stays exact. |
| Tail/read/filter semantics were stronger than primitives | Default reads produce numbered display text and can be capped; literal filtering operates on that representation. Tail's metadata/read calls are best-effort across file changes unless a real retained-file snapshot contract exists; empty tail needs a qualified offset/limit profile with limit0. Regex/files-without-match inverse stays blocked rather than being replaced by substring filtering. |
| Pure wrappers and multi-Tool prose could become invalid Skills | Marked Skill text as drafts requiring exact one-Tool specialization; task workflows stay Recipes and pure transformations stay PythonCode documentation. Create-only/overwrite, append/replace and patch-one/all meanings must match actual effects and concurrency guarantees. |
| Monty conversion checks could occur after lossy conversion | Require Rust typed-node validation before repr/cycle/depth placeholders become strings. CPython syntax/probe checks are documentation evidence only; exact pinned interpreter, source preflight, typed transport and actual adapters need production-path acceptance later. |
| Generic shell/API reuse could broaden effects or arguments | Bind exact reviewed usage/command/endpoint profiles, classify effects, preserve original invocation/effect identity and live policy, reject option-looking parameters, and treat shell/Tier1 model work according to the guides. Generic dispatchers do not approve commands or selectors; Git fetch updates refs and is not a mutation-free query. |
| Placeholder examples could be installed as completed operations | Preparation-only/missing-host/existence/partial-validator/root examples are explicitly draft/blocked. No catalogue activation, name substitution or Tier2 fallback may conceal missing adapters, approvals or behavior. Keep earlier limited production evidence separate from this documentation audit. |

The historical coverage report preserves its original inventory/dispositions and identifies source corrections separately. The implementation ledger must reconcile live installed overrides and exact component/association revisions before any conversion or retirement. No plan table is a database association, approval, registration or retained implementation artifact.

Audit verification: checked section/inventory coverage, local link/anchor targets, JSON/Python example syntax and focused pure-example edge cases under CPython. These checks establish documentation consistency only; Monty/runtime/behavior/activation acceptance remains required by sections 5–7 and the implementation appendix.


## 14. Recipe-derived execution dependencies (2026-10-09)

The [Recipe-by-Recipe coverage ledger](monty.composition.recipes.md) traces 150 legacy seeded Recipe identities, the two newer packaged host workflows and current validator/prefix workflow plans through actual IBS/composition. Legacy source presence is not completeness: text steps are skipped, Skill prose does not insert code, grouped Python bodies lose all but the first in the legacy composer, and ordinary step_entry bindings are empty. The retained path requires explicit one-UUID steps, typed input layouts and approved usage closure. Many repairs only wire existing section1/2 components correctly; they do not need another executor.

The additions below are PythonCode dependencies only. Names are proposed canonical identities, not activated rows or supported new host APIs. R06–R09 include explicitly blocked request-preparation examples plus the separate required primitive-usage steps; preparation must never be counted as completed database/model/child work. Omit trivial preparers where the supported typed layout already supplies the same data. For every required Tool usage, resolve/reuse its actual executable first and qualify its one-Tool Skill association instead of adding a duplicate component. See the individual [R01–R10 implementation sections](monty.composition.implementation.md#recipe-derived-dependencies).

| Component | Purpose | What needs to change / implement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| R01. `proposed-http-body-text` | Extract a complete inline text response for JSON parsing or provider-specific search extraction. | Validate actual body_text, status and truncation fields before returning text; reject saved/binary/missing/partial bodies. Reuse L024 for endpoint-specific status policy and N06 for parsing. |
| R02. `proposed-model-request-data` | Prepare typed task/request/source data for an explicit model usage. | Separate deterministic packet preparation from model execution. Reuse the existing model primitive/entry point after retained Tier1 qualification; never turn skipped text into an implicit model call. |
| R03. `proposed-model-answer-text` | Validate and extract answer text from the qualified existing Kohai completion envelope. | Stop on an error or invalid answer. Keep structured draft parsing and domain validation as later separate steps; answer text is neither executable code nor an effect confirmation. |
| R04. `proposed-trigger-create` | Create one scheduled trigger from an already prepared name, prompt, cron and policy. | Add the missing executable for trigger-create, using the actual primitive parameters and a strict selector guard. Listing/preparation is separate; uncertain persistence or rollback never permits automatic repeat. |
| R05. `proposed-component-content-hash` | Compute a deterministic SHA256 hash of supplied complete document text. | Add the missing one-operation component_db compute_hash usage. Hashing must consume faithful complete text, not numbered/capped read display or a model summary. |
| R06. `proposed-document-hash-request` | Prepare the typed document-name lookup needed for stored-hash comparison. | Add the missing reusable preparation; separately qualify the existing component_db read_hash usage under final-v3 host-derived routing. The current legacy scope argument cannot be invented away or exposed as operator roles. |
| R07. `proposed-pending-document-fields` | Prepare exact typed source or derived documentation fields for pending storage. | Separate document rendering from storage and approval. Reuse the existing pending storage primitive only if its mutable documentation contract fits; it must never write or activate immutable approved executable components. |
| R08. `proposed-prefix-invalidation-request` | Prepare the explicit invalidation request after a confirmed document change. | Make the hidden doc-sync mark-stale action a separate usage. Reuse current publication/generation invalidation when it covers the same effect; never pretend an acknowledged best-effort mark guarantees fresh content. |
| R09. `proposed-child-task-request` | Prepare reusable typed goal/context for a separately admitted child task. | Add the missing preparation and explicit child usage requirement behind subagent Recipes. Verify actual scheduler/admission/wait support; the existing builtin.spawn_subagent authorized:true result is not evidence that a child ran. |
| R10. `proposed-complete-document-text` | Validate a complete faithful raw document snapshot before hashing and conversion. | Add the shared check and separately qualify real raw acquisition/export; numbered read display cannot recover original bytes or newline details. Preserve exact source identity and completeness through doc-sync/doc-convert/prefix export. |

Existing components to wire/review rather than duplicate: L001/L002/L006 for file edits; L017/L019/L022/L023 for HTTP mutations and GitHub writes; N06 for JSON parsing; L028/L029/L031 for memory/plan workflows; L049–L052 for Git effects; L033/L090/L091 for documentation extraction/comparison/rendering; proposed-trigger-remove for ID removal; N01–N04 for root-owned reply/history/routing; N08–N20 for validator/prefix obligations. Requalify existing pc-host-kohai-complete and its primitive for explicit Tier1 child model usage only if its real adapter/association/result/task contract can fit; the current protected root model path remains separate. Reuse R02/R03 only when preparation/result normalization is actually needed. Reuse L027 instead of converting pc-web-search-query-build into a duplicate URL encoder, and L004 instead of duplicating pc-grep-env-files where the exact glob semantics agree.

Skill closure is explicit: each tool-calling executable resolves a reviewed one-Tool prose Skill, ToolSkill, Tool and separate exact-combination approval in the retained dependency graph. Adding prose UUIDs to an executable include would violate one-component steps. Pure helpers have no artificial Skill/ToolSkill. The ledger supplies ordered repair requirements for every Recipe; missing control-flow, complete-source, adapter, evidence or child support blocks activation. Do not route those gaps to Tier2.

Verification for this extension is source and documentation inspection, inventory/link checks and Python example parsing/probes only. No components, Recipe rows, BuildInstructions, runtime code or activated routes were changed; real pinned Monty/production acceptance remains implementation work.


## 15. Migration priority and actual use

See [active wiring versus legacy definitions](monty.composition.recipes.md#active-wiring-versus-retained-legacy-definitions-2026-10-09-follow-up). The legacy composer is outside ordinary global execution but still referenced by an incomplete optional MCP composition handler; replace that handler with the ordinary-chat client integration described in section16, rather than expanding the legacy composer. MCP itself remains a required capability. json-parse and doc-convert remain seeded, but are not selected by the current installed global catalogue. Preserve needed JSON parsing via N06. Reconcile doc-sync's still-wired watcher producers with the v3 prefix/source pipeline and rebuild its needed functionality as a Recipe only where an equivalent canonical workflow is absent. These source defects are migration specifications, not proof of active broken execution or a requirement to reactivate dormant seeds.


## 16. MCP invokes Skill execution Recipes through intent matching

MCP is a **normal-chat client**, not a direct Monty/IBS/Tool execution bridge.
Each eligible Skill has a canonical execution Recipe or equivalent existing
variant. tools/list gives its exact command sentence, variable positions, types,
formatting/escaping rules and examples. The model returns only that completed
command. MCP checks it against the listed command contract, creates a new normal
chat and submits the command as its user message. The existing chat ingress,
global orchestrator, intent matching, IBS and Recipe execution handle it exactly
as another chat message. The result is posted into that chat; MCP forwards the
correlated response to its client, then closes the chat through its ordinary
supported lifecycle operation. MCP accepts no Python source.

### Recipe-derived discovery and Kohai-owned provider connections

**Discovery source:** build MCP tools/list from the available activated, approved
`mcp-call-skill-recipes` and their qualified variants, not directly from raw Skill
rows, Rust Tools or every Recipe. Each listing describes that Recipe's one Skill
usage and derives its exact sentence, variable positions/types, encoding,
examples and public result/error contract from the same approved Recipe/interface
closure. A Skill without an available qualified MCP-call execution Recipe is not
yet advertised. These are purpose/association requirements, not proof that a
name prefix or an invented database flag establishes eligibility. Reuse a
compatible existing Recipe/variant; do not duplicate it merely for discovery.

**Always-on server:** start and supervise one MCP server during ordinary
BrassClaw startup, after supported chat/catalogue readiness. Build and atomically
publish its initial listing before Kohai can advertise it. Keep the server
running through provider disconnects and idle periods until instance shutdown
or supervised server recovery. It serves metadata and ordinary chat operations;
it has no direct Monty/IBS/Rust Tool execution port. A provider connection is not
the server's lifetime, a Monty lifetime or a Tool permission grant.

**Kohai connection window:** finish normal prompt composition, including the
final selected prefix addition. Immediately before sending that prompt to the
model, Kohai establishes the supported provider MCP connection and advertises
the corresponding complete listing. Retain the advertised catalogue/command
contract identity with that provider request. Then send the prompt through the
existing provider adapter. This is connection/advertisement lifecycle wiring,
not another Recipe executor or LLM call. Verify the provider's actual supported
MCP discovery/configuration mechanism; invent no universal connect API or
provider request field. Do not move provider credentials into model prose.

When the complete model answer is received, Kohai disconnects/removes this
request's provider MCP connection and advertisement; the server stays running.
A first streamed token, progress event or intermediate tool invocation is not
the complete answer. Preserve the connection while the exchange still needs
its advertised tools/results. If the provider uses multiple request exchanges,
apply the same compose/connect/advertise/send/answer/disconnect window to each
actual model request with explicit in-flight-call ownership. Failure, timeout,
cancellation and send/composition errors use the same owned cleanup path.
Disconnect affects that request only; concurrent provider requests retain their
own connections. Never disconnect another request or stop the shared server.

**Ordinary chat calls during the window:** MCP accepts only a completed command
from the advertised Recipe list, opens a new normal chat, sends it as a user
message, waits for its correlated terminal response, forwards that response and
closes the chat. Provider disconnection does not prove the command had no effect
or cancel/delete an already admitted chat. Drain or reconcile in-flight calls
through the existing chat lifecycle; retain their result for delivery/recovery
and never reopen another chat to repeat an uncertain/completed command.

**Listing refresh:** rebuild at every BrassClaw start/restart from the coherent
approved catalogue. Subscribe to the supported component activation/catalogue
change path so that adding a callable Skill with its qualified MCP-call Recipe
publishes an updated listing without server restart. Draft/proposal creation
alone does not advertise an unapproved usage. Also refresh after compatible
Recipe/Skill/interface updates, withdrawals or repaired catalogue generations;
reject malformed/colliding commands and report catalogue failures honestly.
Publish the entire new list atomically, not partially while its closure loads.
No new Rust Tool, polling LLM or special component-approval step is required.

New provider requests advertise the refreshed generation. An already advertised
request retains its original command contract; an update must not silently
change its tool meaning. Keep older listing/contract references while in-flight
calls need them. Before ordinary chat submission, verify the advertised command
is still supported by the normal active matcher or reject incompatibility before
effects; do not select an older Recipe secretly or introduce direct pinned
executor access through MCP. Once normal chat matching selects a workflow, its
existing exact task snapshot governs execution, independent of future listing
refreshes and provider disconnects. Live kernel Tool policy remains current.

**Acceptance:** demonstrate server readiness without any connected provider;
listing derived only from qualified MCP-call Recipe variants; initial/restart
rebuild and activation-driven atomic refresh; final prefix -> connection and
advertisement -> prompt send ordering; complete answer -> request-local
disconnect with the server still alive; streamed/tool-round-trip/concurrent
request isolation; timeout/cancel/auth/provider-error cleanup; and in-flight
chat/result recovery without effect replay. Missing provider protocol or chat
support is explicit adapter work, not a substitute direct execution bridge.
This remains a documentation/implementation plan, not implemented acceptance.

### Target sequence and responsibilities

1. **List available MCP-call Recipes.** Project eligible approved
   mcp-call-skill-recipes through supported metadata access, resolving each
   Recipe's associated Skill usage and qualified command variants. Each entry gives the exact sentence
   and variable placement/encoding for a qualified Recipe variant; names are
   unambiguous. Private helpers and protected/internal usages are not exposed.
   Reuse an equivalent execution Recipe rather than allocate a duplicate.
2. **Accept the completed command.** Check the tools/call name against its listed
   sentence, parameter rules and supported contract. Variables may vary only in
   their declared positions and types. Reject extra commands, source-bearing
   payloads and stale/incompatible listed contracts before sending a message.
   These are ordinary request checks, not component Q1/Q2 or arbitrary intent
   inference inside MCP. The existing chat path performs actual intent matching.
3. **Open a chat and submit once.** Use the existing authenticated chat create
   and send-message interface; keep the returned chat/message/run references
   associated with the originating MCP request/client. Send the completed command
   verbatim as one user message, not a system prompt or injected instructions.
   Keep each request's new chat isolated from other clients and ordinary chats.
   MCP neither starts a Monty VM nor invokes a Rust Tool, composer, matcher,
   Python admission port or global child scheduler directly.
4. **Use ordinary chat execution.** The already-running global orchestrator
   receives the message through normal ingress and matches first. The matching
   Recipe is assembled and executed with its existing retained component/export
   selection, bindings, typed data, kernel checks, waits and recovery. No special
   MCP Recipe runner, matcher or chat execution mode is added. Function preloading
   remains the separately documented Skill migration; MCP is only a transport
   consumer of that library.
5. **Collect the chat response.** Observe the owning message/run's normal
   terminal outcome and response through the existing chat event/history API.
   Correlate by identity, not merely the newest message or a piece of text.
   An acknowledgement or progress event is not completion. The ordinary chat
   result/reply/history owner formats and posts the response once; MCP serializes
   that response and its supported outcome into the MCP result. It does not run
   another formatter, reply Tool or history writer.
6. **Close after completion.** After collecting the terminal response and
   retaining the request/result correlation needed for delivery/recovery, close
   the chat using its supported close/archive operation. Closing is lifecycle
   cleanup, not deletion of retained workflow/effect evidence. If close/delivery
   fails, recover that operation from the same chat/result; never create another
   chat and repeat the completed command. Pending/waiting/unknown runs retain
   their chat and use ordinary wait/cancel/reconciliation; a transport timeout
   does not establish completion or permission to discard/replay work.

### Matching, results and authority

Every exposed command must actually match its canonical Skill execution Recipe
through ordinary ingress; qualify positive, invalid and cross-Skill cases. Keep
list/call contract updates coherent. The server verifies only the listed command
shape; it does not decide which Recipe to execute. The existing matcher/IBS owns
selection and its single approved snapshot. Use normal Recipe result-to-chat
formatting, including an existing pure formatter when the usage returns an
object. MCP returns that chat response, not a directly accessed intermediate
Monty value. Domain failures, cancellation and unresolved effects remain their
actual normal-chat outcomes, never success inferred from arbitrary response text.

The ordinary chat routing rules remain unchanged: only genuine No-Match can
enter its existing Tier2 path; matching/DB/assembly/begun execution errors do not.
Listed commands are qualified to match. A stale/misconfigured command reaching
No-Match is a contract failure to repair, not evidence that the requested Skill
ran. Do not add a private MCP No-Match bypass or a second routing policy. Read
supported normal-chat outcome metadata when available; do not invent an API
field to conceal missing observability.

MCP's supported credentials reach only its ordinary chat/catalogue interface,
not a direct executor or root-control API. The server has no additional tool
grants: normal live instance-wide Tool policy and technical restrictions still
apply. This narrows the integration surface and reuses existing enforcement;
it is not proof that chat transport alone eliminates malicious arguments or
makes every operation safe. No new component or per-call Q1/behavior/human Q2
is created by invoking an already approved Recipe. Recipe/interface authoring
retains its separate authored or verified system_seed qualification.

### Required implementation and acceptance

Keep the MCP server always running with ordinary chat create/send/observe/close
abilities and Recipe-derived list projection/command checking. Kohai owns only
request-local provider connections/advertisement and cleanup; catalogue changes
refresh the list through supported activation events. Replace its
obsolete Skill-UUID -> empty-step_link composer caller. **Do not add a direct
MCP-to-Monty/Rust Tool/IBS/global-child bridge or change the existing execution
architecture for MCP.** Inspect actual chat lifecycle/auth/result interfaces
before wiring the server; this plan invents no endpoints or request fields.
Missing ordinary chat support is addressed in its existing product owner, not
with another execution loop. Existing Skill/function/library qualification gaps
remain their own migration work, not new MCP execution infrastructure.

Acceptance must demonstrate list -> valid completed command -> fresh chat ->
one normal user message -> ordinary matched Recipe -> correlated terminal chat
response -> MCP result -> chat closure. Also cover invalid/stale/cross-Skill
commands, concurrent chats, chat creation/send acknowledgement uncertainty,
waits, cancellation, disconnects, response delivery and close failures. Preserve
request/chat/message/run/attempt references and confirmed/unresolved effects;
never resend an uncertain message or replay a completed write after a lost reply.
The current listener/stub is not acceptance of this transport.

See [the per-Skill execution Recipe instructions](monty.composition.skill-recipes.md).
Plans only; no chat/runtime implementation or activation is performed here.

## 17. Recipe coverage with composer, doc-convert and json-parse assumed correct

The [counterfactual ledger](monty.composition.recipes.md#counterfactual-re-audit-with-the-three-legacy-defects-removed) rechecks every one of the 151 old Recipe identities and the newer/planned workflows. Assume composition correctly assembles explicitly declared code, typed edges, bindings and Skill associations, JSON parsing really parses, and both document conversion variants perform their promised conversion with the necessary executable closure. These fixes do not invent other workflows from prose, make an absent child/validator adapter exist, or qualify every legacy association. The earlier actual-defect audit remains separate.

Most required executables already exist or are already covered by L001–L101, N01–N20, the split helpers and R01–R10. Missing references must be wired to those components, not treated as a need for another file, HTTP, shell, JSON, model or child executor. In particular, JSON and document conversion defects add **no new components in this re-audit**. R05–R08/R10 retain their earlier document/refresh dispositions; conversion-internal work is supplied by the hypothesis, whereas external doc-sync refresh effects remain a separate workflow.

The following five small PythonCode parts close or make explicit the remaining reusable logic. All are pure and therefore require no artificial Skill or ToolSkill. C03 is conditional on structured-result formatting; C04/C05 are required only for verified line-comment output. Their [implementation sections](monty.composition.implementation.md#counterfactual-recipe-dependencies-c01c05) give contracts, code and Recipe wiring.

| Component | Purpose | Required implementation/change |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C01 `proposed-api-absolute-url` | Combine an approved API origin with an encoded relative request path. | Extract the reusable origin/path assembly missing from web-search and prose-only GitHub usages. Reuse existing encoders and path preparation; do not add another HTTP executor. |
| C02 `proposed-patch-precondition` | Check that a proposed replacement targets the intended text without ambiguity. | Add the missing reusable pre-effect check for file patches, memory patches and plan marker updates. Reuse existing patch executables; require freshness enforcement separately. |
| C03 `proposed-finding-text` | Render one structured review finding with severity, explanation and proposed fix. | Supply explicit reusable formatting where a Recipe selects structured findings. A model returning an already complete answer can bypass this helper; do not call formatting a missing model executor. |
| C04 `proposed-text-diff-anchors` | Derive eligible file line and side targets from a complete ordinary unified text diff. | Add conservative deterministic hunk accounting for verified line comments. Unsupported or invalid diff forms stop. A general-comment branch needs a separately qualified known-unsupported outcome; C04 does not supply that classifier. |
| C05 `proposed-pr-line-comment` | Prepare a GitHub review comment for a verified target in the reviewed commit. | Validate diff identity, head freshness observation and the chosen anchor, then prepare typed API fields. Reuse the existing POST usage under its exact comment association. |

**Overlap disposition:** C01 extracts assembly already embedded in L076–L079 and some Zencoder helpers; reuse it where contracts match rather than retaining parallel URL builders. C02 supplies the prerequisite shared by L006/L030/L089; those components remain the single patch dispatch implementations. C03 specializes the existing line-reply formatter's input semantics and must not duplicate it for simple line lists. C04 produces anchors and C05 consumes them; neither repeats L080–L082 diff acquisition or L017 HTTP dispatch. Keep existing stable identities where a compatible successor profile can absorb these parts; proposed names are roles, not new assigned UUIDs.

Skill coverage is a separate obligation: each reused Tool-calling entry point must retain its exact one-usage Skill and ToolSkill/Tool association. GitHub metadata/diff/content GET and create-PR/general-comment/line-comment POST are distinct reviewed parameter/result profiles even if they reuse L016/L017. The existing Skill companion drafts should be specialized and associated, not copied into new prose-only Skills. Requalify the existing model usage for explicit Tier1 work. No healthy composer can turn a domain overview into the complete set of one-Tool usages.

Qualification of raw reads, validator fact/behavior producers, prefix export/compiler/store profiles, child admission/waits and MCP command-to-Recipe invocation remains the prerequisite work already recorded in this plan. These are not five newly discovered generic executors. The hypothetical audit does not establish active catalogue eligibility or runtime acceptance.


## 18. Recipe components requiring v3 upgrade or completion

This is the requested Recipe-only list: component, purpose and short change description. It covers all **151 legacy seeded Recipe identities**, two packaged successor workflows, eight class-specific validator successors and the explicitly planned prefix/refresh workflow roles below. MCP chat transport is an integration requirement in section16, not an additional Recipe component. The ground truth is [recipe.md](../../recipe.md), [skills.md](../../skills.md) and [validator_v3.md](../../validator_v3.md); no validator.md exists in this checkout. Tools/ToolSkills/PythonCode are dependencies, not extra Recipe entries. Actions such as doc-sync appear only as a proposed replacement Recipe role. Proposed workflow labels below identify design roles, not claims that an assigned class21 UUID or runnable Recipe already exists.

**List inclusion and common changes.** Every legacy row needs v3 conversion/retirement review because its source does not establish the complete qualified workflow. Each row inherits: actual persisted knowledge/stepnumber schema and valid variant/step_link; one UUID per component step; immediately paired ToolSkill/PythonCode steps; explicit one-usage Skill associations; recursive typed inputs/results and exact edges; one coherent task-start selection with retained exact code/Tool artifacts/association approvals; supported child/wait/resume state; correct tier; explicit completion/reply/history owner; classified failure/cancellation/retry and durable effect handling; trusted bootstrap versus authored review evidence. Public variants need positive/negative intent/input cases, at least ten Recipe intent examples and whole-workflow behavior acceptance. Internal validator/root routes instead have a qualified typed internal entry point and are excluded from public matching. Actual support must be proven before activation.

The change column highlights the usage-specific work rather than repeating this gate. The [full source ledger](monty.composition.recipes.md#each-legacy-recipe) and [counterfactual ledger](monty.composition.recipes.md#counterfactual-re-audit-with-the-three-legacy-defects-removed) supply exact dependency/defect details; all earlier L/N/R/C components were checked for reuse. Unlike section17's hypothetical question, this upgrade list includes the actual JSON/conversion/composition repairs.

**Overlap interpretation.** “Review overlap” marks both members of a possible Recipe consolidation, not an automatic deletion. Compare operation, captures/defaults, result/effects, failure contract, tier and intent boundaries; prefer an added intent/compatible variant before a separate Recipe. Different outputs/selectors can justify separate variants or Recipes. Sharing PythonCode/Skills alone is normal reuse and is not proof of duplicate Recipe functionality. Newer packaged/validator successors retain their canonical stable identities/revisions where compatible; do not install old and new copies as separate competing matches.

## 19. Implementation instructions for every listed Recipe

The [per-Recipe implementation appendix](monty.composition.recipe-implementation.md) contains RCP001–RCP170, one section for each section18 entry, including legacy/successor overlap dispositions. Each section gives purpose, concrete component sequence, code example, input/behavior requirements, persisted IBS schema example and completion/acceptance instructions. UUID resolution, supported typed layout, actual blocked adapters and exact qualification remain explicitly required; examples do not activate components. Superseded protected-root roles have migration instructions rather than fabricated empty runnable Recipes.


## 20. Correctness, security and v3/Monty alignment audit

Audit scope: all five monty.composition documents, the four binding component guides and validator_v3, the selected host/retained-composition contracts, and the official Monty documentation. Corrections below are **plan changes only**. The 170 per-Recipe sections remain draft designs; 166 illustrative JSON skeletons and four protected-root retirement sections are not activated components or proof of full catalogue support.

### Additional reusable PythonCode required by the audited designs

| Component | Purpose | Required implementation and overlap disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [C06. proposed-document-render](monty.composition.implementation.md#c06-proposed-document-render) | Produce the complete derived document from its separately rendered header and extracted or compressed body. | Add the small pure join with a single-line nonblank header contract. Both doc-convert variants reuse it before derived hashing/storage. L091 already formats the header and R05/R07 cover hash/store roles; reuse those identities and do not duplicate them. Keep raw source content/hash and confirmed parent receipt separate. |
| [C07. proposed-typed-json-text](monty.composition.implementation.md#c07-proposed-typed-json-text) | Render an approved finite typed public result as JSON text for a string-consuming reply or protocol profile. | Use the supported Monty json.dumps profile with allow_nan=False and a strict recursive input boundary. Reuse L073 where its actual typed-object adapter matches; its string-reparse semantics differ from this profile. Reuse existing text/line formatters when they fit. **Review old L073 before allocating an identity**, and add only the distinct reusable profile; do not expose internal/secret-bearing envelopes or turn failures into success. |

### Findings addressed in the component and Recipe instructions

1. **Preparation counted as execution.** L018 was marked pure in download Recipe designs because its blocked example returns request fields. Both download variants now require the real save usage's binding/executor/association and actual HTTP/write receipt; an ordinary GET never saves. The common typo referring to L019 preparation is corrected to L018.
2. **HTTP/body/model profiles and copied examples drifted.** L016 now binds headers and a finite response limit. Per-Recipe code/input tables were refreshed from the canonical definitions. Declare exact media/auth/body profiles: the actual HTTP adapter serializes a typed JSON body but treats text as raw UTF-8; a string-only profile must explicitly serialize first. R02's data packet is not the current Kohai prompt schema; the plan requires an exact typed prompt mapping and task-pinned complete prefix, without latest/minimal fallback or invented host arguments.
3. **Incomplete data edges.** Both conversion variants now explicitly acquire/hash/store the original, render header plus body with C06, hash that exact derived content and store it with the confirmed source ID. Each write retains its own receipt. Doc-sync validates the child-owned store receipts and performs only its separately required invalidation; the parent never repeats those writes. Public structured results use a selected formatter before the one string reply owner; C07 fills the demonstrated scalar/string serialization gap. No implicit repr, prose execution or automatic model summarization supplies missing code.
4. **Incorrect search/control assumptions.** Ranked memory search reuses L025 selection and branches on no hit. File creation/replacement, PR pagination/content/finding iterations, compression/extraction, validator conditional reasoning, doc-sync and prefix waits and ordinary-chat waits observed by MCP require supported retained control-tree/occurrence/continuation contracts. The straight skeletons explicitly do not implement those flows. Unknown/missing binding support blocks activation rather than flattening effects or inventing persisted branch fields.
5. **Candidate and review evidence conflated.** N16 remains immutable candidate proposal admission. Validators' complete N15 outcome goes to the trusted review owner for verified Q1 persistence through its actual profile. Prefix evaluation also needs its actual evidence owner, not a candidate sink. Authored human Q2 and activation remain distinct; verified system_seed qualification remains noncircular and needs no additional instance Q2. Neither individual component approval nor a selection manifest establishes exact-combination approval.
6. **Diff/plan parsing could invent physical lines.** C04 and L089 now split physical LF lines instead of treating Unicode separators as line boundaries. C04 rejects repeated files, overlapping/reordered hunks and stray/repeated no-newline markers, accepts function text containing @@, and binds both reviewed base and head. C05 checks independent base/head freshness observations and one exact anchor before posting. Unsupported or invalid forms stop; a general-comment fallback needs a separately qualified known-unsupported classifier. Shell acquisition uses the actual output field. These checks do not create an atomic PR head lock or filesystem precondition lock.
7. **Readiness overstated by example completeness.** Every runnable-design section now says DRAFT. Stable UUID placeholders, empty binding/capture arrays and linked contract prose are unresolved authoring work, not insert requests. Complete finite schemas, exact retained layouts, bindings/associations, all variant/child dependencies, routing and behavioral qualification must be supplied and checked on the real path before activation. Retained old task selections/artifacts stay usable; corruption/failure in an active catalogue remains an error, never Tier2.

### Monty-specific implementation gates

The selected dependency is the local Rust [monty-control package](../../vendor/monty-control/BRASSCLAW.md): upstream v1.0.0 commit 85c5d1f6bef038405cfc40a4eed94806e303567e, extension 1.0.0-brassclaw.control.7 and dump ABI 0xBC04. Recheck these exact identities before implementation. Upstream Python/JavaScript session/pool examples do not establish that BrassClaw's retained adapter implements the same APIs. Monty is a Python interpreter; VM refers to that execution machinery. Upstream worker pools provide their own process APIs; BrassClaw does not inherit those APIs merely by using the Rust interpreter. The current [startup](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/global_monty_startup.rs) and [GlobalProcess](../../crates/brassclaw_monty_host/src/process.rs) host the global root in one privately spawned worker executable with framed IPC, a cleared environment and configured physical/frame backstops. That worker is an implementation isolation boundary, not a VM per task or proof of complete production recovery/settings/catalogue acceptance. The one global service/task isolation target is unchanged. See [Monty overview](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/).

BrassClaw's step-local inputs mapping, host.<tool> call surface and result capture are adapter contracts, not names automatically supplied by upstream Monty. Validate exact assembled code/declarations and its retained dependency graph under the selected engine. Optional type checking and stubs must reflect the real host interfaces; they supplement runtime recursive contracts and never establish behavior, approval or authority. Upstream eval/exec availability is distinct from BrassClaw's policy forbidding dynamic runtime source. Limit accepted modules/callables to the qualified component profile even where upstream offers more. See [host functions](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/host-functions/), [type checking](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/type-checking/), [limitations](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/) and [JSON limitations](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/json/).

Upstream eager iterators/generator expressions and interpreter allocations need finite admitted values and supported resource accounting. Feed/turn limits do not by themselves implement BrassClaw's cumulative executing-time account across steps/feeds/children/waits; preserve that account and effective setting revision, excluding idle/external waits. Interpreter allocation limits do not bound process RSS, compilation or host callback memory/work. Host callbacks need their own kernel/transport technical limits, cancellation and current global policy. An interpreter-terminal heap/time/control failure fences the affected global generation and enters supervised reconciled recovery; do not assume a damaged global heap can continue or silently spawn a per-chat replacement. See [resource limits](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/resource-limits/) and [security](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/security/), together with the local control extension's task-preemption/terminal-error contract.

Persisted Monty snapshot bytes are not inherently authenticated. Accept only a trusted producer/storage boundary with authenticated integrity and compatible exact engine/control ABI and retained workflow/state references; an attacker-controlled blob plus its checksum is insufficient. Never accept snapshots from model/MCP input or treat a restored callback/authority object as a grant. Restore controls/resource settings and trusted host objects through the qualified recovery owner, reconcile durable effects/counts and fence the old generation before supervised replacement. A heap dump is not a durable workflow checkpoint. See [snapshots](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/snapshots/).

### Verification required and evidence limits

Recheck every executable example under the exact retained Monty source parser, actual typed input/result conversion and allowed host declarations; verify each completed Recipe variant/control/layout/association closure on the production path. Focused local pure-function examples must cover physical-line spoofing, duplicate/overlapping diff subjects, source/base/head mismatch, header/body preservation, scalar JSON-looking text and nonfinite rejection. Structural Markdown/JSON/Python checks only show that these plan examples are well formed; they do not qualify a component, prove semantic review or implement missing runtime support. No Cargo build, component activation or runtime implementation is part of this audit.


## 21. Tools requiring v3 reconciliation, retention or qualification

This list and section22 extend the plan to **Tools (class0)** and **ToolSkills (class13)**. They are separate from PythonCode and Recipe inventory counts. Authority is [recipe.md](../../recipe.md), [skills.md](../../skills.md), [tools.md](../../tools.md), [toolskills.md](../../toolskills.md) and [validator_v3.md](../../validator_v3.md); no validator.md exists in this checkout. This is a source audit of bundled definitions, registered handlers, packaged successors and explicit planned usages, not a live database/usage report.

**Inclusion rule:** include a legacy/current definition when its source/registered schema, binding, effect/result/failure, immutable implementation, policy identity or supported combination needs reconciliation; include a newer same-identity successor to record the overlap and remaining scope; include a registered primitive missing its supported library identity where a consumer needs it; identify a planned primitive only as a conditional gap, never an invented implemented Tool. All 32 unique class0 seed names and 46 unique class13 seed names in builtin_bootstrap, seed_builtin_host and zencoder_bootstrap were examined, including the two prefix Tools and the eight helper-built Zencoder descriptors. Repeated seeding of memory_write is one identity. Four additional Tool and four ToolSkill archive-only proposals (Skill listing/install/removal and host regex matching) were separately compared with the current registry; schemas or historical descriptions do not prove they remain installed. New packaged rows reuse existing Tool UUIDs; profile labels below allocate no UUID.

**Shared Tool change for every legacy row:** resolve stable UUID -> every capability ID/alias -> actual Monty callable/adapter -> retained native/artifact implementation; reconcile registration and immutable revision selection with one approved task-start snapshot. Validate registered inputs against Rust decoding and finite recursive usage contracts, actual payload/error/effect certainty and technical limits. Current live instance-wide Tool policy applies to every retained alias/version before dispatch; preserve cancellation, durable attempt counts/effect reconciliation and technical auth/sandbox/network/secret enforcement. Never infer safe retry from GET/read labels, loader readiness from a row/build, or exact-combination approval from validated/source labels. Use noncircular verified system_seed qualification for bundled successors and authored Q1/behavior/humanQ2 for installed edits. These shared changes are part of each row even where the primitive itself needs no redesign.

Current [native registration](../../crates/brassclaw_host_runtime/src/native_registration.rs) captures an actual loaded image and input/adapter declaration; [installed_monty_catalogue](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/installed_monty_catalogue.rs) retains packaged reply/history workflows. This is narrower than general approved library loading, version/alias retention and authored catalogue activation. The [legacy Tool store](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/pg_tool_store.rs) still has scoped insert/no-overwrite and system-label status behavior; it is not the full final-v3 immutable authoring/approval path. Do not erase working supported machinery or add fictitious constructor fields to cover gaps.

Classify every overlap before consolidation: same stable Tool with successor revisions; compatible bindings for the same operation; partial infrastructure reuse; or complementary operations. Only the first two can justify identity/profile reuse after exact compatibility checks. Overlap means a required review, not automatic deletion: a Tool and its own bindings/Skills/code/Recipes are complementary roles, not duplicates. Distinct methods/bodies/adapters can legitimately share one primitive. Keep old selected artifacts/records until tasks no longer need them. Reconcile any duplicate dispatch identity fail-closed before activation; never silently downgrade or turn an active corruption/error into No-Match/Tier2.

## 22. ToolSkills requiring v3 reconciliation, association or binding qualification

The list contains each legacy descriptor separately, the two packaged bindings, two typed guide designs and the planned usage profiles needed by the existing component/Recipe plans. Planned/unresolved labels are **binding requirements**, not proof of newly seeded class13 identities or host API names. Confirm each profile against existing descriptors before allocation. Reuse the stable descriptor if compatible; create a distinct one only for a real parameter/adapter/result compatibility requirement. A new Recipe phrase, order or local input name does not require another ToolSkill.

**Shared ToolSkill change for every legacy row:** replace executable/Orchestrator/workflow instructions with nonexecuting one-Tool binding metadata. Resolve exact Tool UUID, callable/capability/adapter/retained artifact and argument/result/error/effect/prerequisite contract. Store actual supported structured records and exact Skill/PythonCode/ToolSkill/Tool associations plus separate exact-combination approval. Metadata templates never paste runtime values into source, execute a call, select workflow or grant permission. Inputs use supported recursive finite schemas and exact missing/default/null/extra/numeric rules; registered JSON Schema and usage ValueSchema are different formats that must agree. Bind one UUID in a Rust Recipe step immediately before its matching one-UUID class22 usage; independent calls remain separate. Keep approved originals/selected combinations and live Tool policy checks.

The [current legacy descriptor constructor](../../crates/brassclaw_reborn_composition/src/pg_tool_skill_store.rs) has tool_name, param_schema, param_template, content/includes and optional content_checksum; those fields do not themselves supply the full binding/approval/retained implementation contract. Current [packaged descriptor documents](../../crates/brassclaw_skills/src/global_bootstrap_components.rs) actually use **tool-skill-binding/1** for the narrow retained path; this is source evidence, not a new universally supported legacy insert API or the guide's rejected invented toolskill-binding/1 spelling. Inspect actual consumers before reuse. Binding never executes text or requires a Rust compilation merely to author metadata.

TS069 is a family of unresolved inspection requirements, not an allocated generic inspection descriptor: source read, manifest export and registration facts each need their actual one-operation binding if they dispatch independently. One label does not permit several independent calls in an executable step.

On the qualified typed path, invalid/null values must not be repaired by the legacy host's optional-null/string-"null" normalization. Declare omission/default rules and validate before that compatibility adaptation; a literal string "null" remains data when valid. Error-policy labels ignore/retry/fallback do not establish final failure semantics. Default stop/one attempt until exact safety evidence supports another contract; no replay of a confirmed effect after output, history or evidence persistence fails.

### Coverage and disposition boundaries

The Tool list has 44 entries: 32 seeded definitions, four archive-only proposals, two same-Tool packaged revisions, four registered trigger primitives with no matching class0 seed found, one conditional compiler-worker primitive and the legacy unseeded run_program bridge. These are not 44 new Tool UUIDs. The ToolSkill list has 75 entries: 46 seeded descriptors, four archive-only proposals, four current-trigger binding gaps, two packaged bindings, two guide profiles and 17 planned/unresolved profiles. Several can resolve to the same compatible existing identity. Both directions of each identified old/new overlap are marked; related but non-equivalent create/list/read/write operations are not silently merged.

The four newer trigger handlers need compatible binding/Skill/code profiles only if their functionality is brought into the Recipe catalogue; no class13 seed for them was found. Complete their actual Tool identity/registered input/result contracts first, then add the necessary approved usage profiles without implementing another scheduler. Their four planned binding profiles are included above as conditional follow-up component requirements, not accepted callables from this plan.

N19 and other pure validators/formatters require no fictional ToolSkill. Actual trusted inspection, behavior observation and evidence persistence use separately qualified primitives. Root-only transport, stream/admission/lifecycle ports, underscore legacy helpers, native registration/compilation infrastructure, external MCP service schemas and ordinary Rust helper functions are not automatically class0/class13 library components. Their support requirements remain in the global/Recipe/validator plans; do not create a binding for every Rust function or expose root authority to a child. MCP lists Orchestrator Skills and submits their completed Recipe command as a user message in a new ordinary chat; these lists do not change that boundary.

Implementation order: reconcile canonical identities and current handlers first; repair metadata/operation/result/effect mismatches second; complete compatible finite bindings and exact associations/approvals third; validate each consuming Recipe's selected layout/control/closure and retained implementation fourth. Qualify via the actual host/kernel/Monty path before coherent activation. Plan tables, parser checks and successful native registration alone are not behavioral acceptance or completed library migration. No implementation, seeding, registration or activation is authorized by this planning update.

## 23. Tool/ToolSkill and cross-plan audit corrections

This audit rechecked the five composition plans against the four binding guides, validator_v3 and the selected local Monty package, using upstream documentation as language/security evidence rather than proof of local adapter support. Sections21–22 remain inventories of definitions and unresolved profiles, not installed catalogue acceptance.

1. **Independent operations were conflated.** TS072 now describes evaluation invocation only; TS074 separately describes trusted evaluation-evidence persistence. TS073 describes profile registration only; TS075 separately describes serving-generation selection. Their independent calls require separate binding/executable steps, exact associations and receipts. TS069 is explicitly a requirement family until its actual individual primitive profiles are resolved. These corrections apply to every prefix/evaluation/validator Recipe instruction in the appendices; no new UUID or callable is asserted.
2. **Overlap language was too broad.** Composition versus delegated execution and shell versus non-shell compilation are complementary or partial reuse, not equivalent operations. Existing Tool UUID successors remain the same Tool; narrower bindings may remain distinct when real compatibility requires it. Candidate admission, trusted inspection, behavioral evidence, Q1 persistence, profile registration and serving selection cannot replace each other merely because they read or write related records. Keep all old selected artifacts and do not delete a component solely from these review flags.
3. **Model admission was overrestricted in prose.** MCP sends only the listed Skill's completed execution Recipe command through a new ordinary chat; matching and execution create no component and accept no Python source. Canonical approved code and existing exact associations are reused; dispatch checks current kernel policy. Model output cannot grant authority, change a pinned task graph, manufacture evidence or silently install/activate persistent components. Tier0 remains model-free execution of qualified associated code.
4. **Catchable host errors need an authoritative outcome.** Monty can deliver host exceptions to Python; a catch block returning a dictionary cannot erase kernel denial, stale/cancelled dispatch, exhausted task budgets or a recorded unresolved/failed effect. Preserve those outcomes outside Python result interpretation and stop or follow only the explicitly qualified failure continuation. Ordinary declared recoverable errors may be handled only by the usage's approved contract. Never interpret an arbitrary success-shaped Python result as proof that the host operation succeeded. Current adapter enforcement must be demonstrated, not assumed. See [host functions](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/host-functions/) and [security](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/concepts/security/).
5. **Host conversion and secrets remain boundary work.** Validate values before a lossy host conversion can stringify unsupported objects or truncate recursive/deep structures; reject values outside the finite approved profile rather than validating the transformed substitute. Supply only public nonsecret headers as task inputs; resolve credentials in the protected host auth boundary, excluding them from code, model context, results, evidence and snapshots. Opaque technical fencing handles are admitted by their trusted owner and checked against the owning task; they are not model-created grants. Every cached callable/alias still receives the same live policy check on dispatch. Upstream host-function proxies resolve by name against the current feed, so a reused name must resolve to the task's retained implementation rather than a newly installed handler. Validate raw Rust MontyNode kinds before lossy JSON conversion; upstream Python/JavaScript conversion examples do not establish local Rust behavior. See [host-value limitations](https://pydantic.dev/docs/monty/limitations/host-values/).
6. **Retaining an image is insufficient to restore a native handler.** Capturing the current executable image/checksum is narrow implementation identity evidence. It is not a cross-version vtable loader. Before an upgrade/recovery can execute an old selection, prove the exact old artifact is actually loadable through a supported compatible ABI/runtime and qualified registration path. If that path is missing, block the upgrade/admission that would strand retained work; never substitute the newest native handler or silently drop the old task. Keep the single supervised global-service lifecycle and reconcile effects before recovery. Restoring an upstream suspension can announce its host call again; intercept that announcement using durable dispatch identity and confirmed/unresolved state. A confirmed effect returns its retained receipt without redispatch; an unresolved effect requires reconciliation, never automatic replay. This is implementation work required by [tools.md](../../tools.md#7-immutable-implementations-and-live-settings), not an additional external approval for bundled seeds.

**Plan acceptance checks:** reconcile every role to one actual operation and compatible finite contract; verify binding adjacency/association/closure and exact artifacts; exercise invalid inputs, policy change between binding and dispatch, caught host denial, unknown effect, cancelled/stale dispatch, evidence-write failure after confirmed evaluation and old-version recovery on the real retained Monty path. Documentation/JSON/parser checks are preliminary evidence only. This audit changes plans; it neither implements these gates nor claims runtime acceptance.

## 24. Preloadable Skill interface migration

1. Rewrite every real Skill's immutable prose/interface with exported names/signatures, canonical code UUID/implementation symbols, exact parameter/result/failure contracts and private dependencies. Preserve the one-Tool scope and existing duplicate reviews; pure-only wrappers are retired as Skills, not fabricated exports.
2. Convert selected class-22 Tool-usage bodies into preloadable function definitions with explicit inputs and returns. Retain small shared helpers as declared PythonCode dependencies; create no duplicate body merely for another compatible public alias. Specialize fixed selectors and incompatible operation profiles before exposing them.
3. Implement and qualify the retained interface reader, dependency-first loader, collision-safe task export environment and transitive function-call inspection in their existing owners. Preloading must produce zero Tool/model/I/O effects, including defaults/initializers. Current top-level one-call/result inspection is narrow historical support, not proof of this new interface.
4. Assemble Recipe invocation steps referencing the canonical class-22 UUID and supported retained export/input selection. Rust ToolSkill binding remains immediately before the invocation. Keep unchanged persisted knowledge/stepnumber grammar and one UUID per step; no invented export/module fields or effectful load steps.
5. Pin interface/source/dependency/association/approval/artifact and symbol resolution from one coherent approved generation. Keep old and new generations available without namespace overwrite; children, waits, retries and resumes retain exact original resolution. Sharing code must never share another task's inputs, arrays, results, closure state or host context.
6. Provide a canonical execution Recipe for each real Skill, reusing an equivalent existing variant. MCP projects its matching command and typed parameters; the ordinary chat path runs matching/IBS and the Recipe's preloaded canonical function, posts its response in that chat and MCP forwards it before closing the chat. No Python source is accepted. It creates no component and triggers no per-call Q1/Q2. Existing inputs/results, live Tool policy and technical limits remain effective.
7. Qualify migration through the actual global Monty path with effect-free load, dependency/collision rejection, repeated/concurrent call isolation, policy change after preload and old/new version coexistence/recovery. New authored interfaces/code need their exact combination review; shipped successors use verified noncircular system_seed qualification. Retain old task selections and report missing support honestly.

Every Skill subsection in the component appendix now has concrete export/alias, implementation reuse or blocked disposition, dependency/load/pin/state instructions and acceptance cases. Every Recipe appendix entry specifies function preloading and invocation. Existing draft limitations are preserved: a preparation dictionary, pure helper, missing adapter or retired root port does not become an executable Skill because it is wrapped in a function.
